```json
          " \\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \\cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d$\\rightarrow$PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \\cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).\\looseness=-1",

          "+\\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} Against BGE-large~\\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB (1/4 precision); R1-8B shows adverse FPR direction (+1.2pp). The advantage is encoder-conditional (\\cref{fig:forest_precision}); layer selection (Contribution 1) is encoder-independent.\\looseness=-1",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Position mediation.} Position ($t/T$) accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy,\\footnote{R1-32B's 20.4pp drop reflects $W{=}1$; at $W{\\geq}3$, drops are 9--17pp.} but all models retain non-positional signal above chance (all $p{<}0.001$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage (residualized gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B's gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$, $n{=}20$). Adding a quadratic position term $(t/T)^2$ does not change significance conclusions for the 7--8B models. However, QwQ-32B's advantage is fully absorbed by the quadratic term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), suggesting its residualized advantage contains a non-linear position component. Temporal windowing reduces position dependence (24.3pp drop at $W{=}3$ vs.\\ 16.5pp at $W{=}15$).\\looseness=-1",
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   Continuing execution of Director's corrected 3-item patch instruction: "[Director 指令] 数字已到位，直接执行 3 项补入". The specific items are:
   - Add Intro §1 hook: "the same model and data yield a 16-layer (R1-8B) to 59-layer (QwQ-32B) divergence depending solely on metric choice"
   - Update Discussion §5 with Director's exact random-CP text (80% crossing rate shallow / 20% precision deep)
   - Add §4.2 split-half sentence referencing `\cref{app:split_half}`
   - Compile and verify 8-page EMNLP limit
   - Git commit and report to Director via `paper_agent_post_to_director`

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 strict 8-page main text limit (Limitations/references exempt)
   - `\looseness=-1` LaTeX trick to compress paragraphs by one line
   - Two-column ACL format with ~12-13 words per column line
   - Metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution 1) vs HS advantage (Contribution 2)
   - Random CP control: pure crossing rate 100% shallow, CR-composite 80% shallow, precision 20% deep under random labels
   - Split-half internal validity on OT-7B: CR→L0, Precision→L20/L26
   - QwQ-32B layer gap: L4 vs L63 = 59 layers (NOT 61)
   - The `paper_compile_and_check` tool counts total pages (including appendix), so it always reports "FAIL: pages 30 > limit 8" — visual inspection of pages 8-9 is required to verify main text fits

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex`** (line 5)
     - Added 16-59 layer divergence hook per Director's instruction
     - Also added `\looseness=-1` for page compression
     ```latex
     We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion---the same model and data yield a 16-layer (R1-8B) to 59-layer (QwQ-32B) divergence depending solely on metric choice.\looseness=-1
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex`** (line 4)
     - Replaced random-CP parenthetical with Director's exact text about 80%/20% numbers
     - Shortened "architecture-independent across Llama and Qwen (R1-8B full-layer sweep; \cref{tab:r18b_full_layer})" to "architecture-independent across Llama/Qwen (\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer})" for page limit
     ```latex
     Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), architecture-independent across Llama/Qwen (\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}). A random-label control on QwQ-32B reveals crossing rate retains its shallow preference in 80\% of permutations while precision's deep preference collapses to 20\% (\cref{app:random_cp}), confirming metric disagreement reflects true CP structure rather than intrinsic metric geometry. Contribution~1 (layer bias) thus generalizes from geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers peak at L63/64~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B selectivity is 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold~\citep{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/sections/experiments.tex`**
     - Added split-half sentence in §4.2 after control task selectivity line:
     ```latex
     Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}). A split-half analysis on OT-7B confirms internal validity (\cref{app:split_half}).\looseness=-1
     ```
     - Added `\looseness=-1` to multiple paragraphs for page compression:
       - §4.2 Capacity control (line 67, after "BGE-large (1/4)")
       - §4.2 Encoder robustness (line 69, after "encoder-independent")

     ```latex
     \section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
     
     Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse conclusions; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) mitigates this. Contribution~1 generalizes geometrically; Contribution~2 is cross-validated at 7--8B, exploratory at 32B$^\dagger$, and encoder-conditional.\looseness=-1
     ```
     - Changes from original: removed "the audit protocol would have caught our spurious finding" → "multi-metric audit protocol mitigates this"; removed "(layer bias)" and "(HS advantage)" labels; changed "but exploratory at 32B and narrow with stronger encoders" → ", exploratory at 32B†, and encoder-conditional"; removed citation `\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti}`

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom~\\citep{gelman2013garden}.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), architecture-independent across Llama/Qwen (\\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}). A random-label control on QwQ-32B reveals crossing rate retains its shallow preference in 80\\% of permutations while precision's deep preference collapses to 20\\% (\\cref{app:random_cp}), confirming metric disagreement reflects true CP structure rather than intrinsic metric geometry. Contribution~1 (layer bias) thus generalizes from geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers peak at L63/64~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B selectivity is 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold~\\citep{hewitt2019control}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (cross-validated at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Whether stronger encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,boxo2025linear_probes} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 (QwQ-32B absorbed by $(t/T)^2$; \\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$, so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution; HS probes partly functioning as better position encoders remains unexcluded. We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility,app:deployment_operating_points}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Layer ensemble proof of concept.}\nTraining probes at the best layer per threshold-based metric (precision, BA, FPR) and averaging predictions yields a robust, metric-agnostic strategy: HS vs.\\ text precision is significant for all four models ($p \\leq 0.002$; \\cref{tab:ensemble}), with +10.5 to +27.0pp advantages. The ensemble does not improve over oracle single-metric selection ($p > 0.27$) but eliminates metric pre-selection.\\looseness=-1\n",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex"
  },
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "c36dcd9c-f257-40ed-baa1-961a2c550ac3",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nReasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before retrospective characterization can inform future detection systems, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides retrospective classification (not online detection) of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect; false positive rates remain 11--50\\%, so deployment applications are future work.\n\nExisting safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion---the same model and data yield a 16-layer (R1-8B) to 59-layer (QwQ-32B) divergence depending solely on metric choice.\\looseness=-1\n\nWe present an initial characterization across four reasoning models---primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models), with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence---at two scales (7--8B, 32B) on the HarmThoughts benchmark. Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric entirely determines whether an HS advantage is observed. A composite metric (balanced accuracy $\\times$ crossing rate) selects shallow layers for 32B models because shallow representations compress directional information, so a linear probe operates near the noise floor and threshold crossings reflect noise rather than semantic transitions. At these layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage.\n\nThreshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge; BA independently confirms deep-layer selection (\\cref{tab:layer_selection}). At these layers, the HS advantage is graded: \\emph{cross-validated} at 7--8B (precision +20.1 to +21.9pp, nested CV; \\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), \\emph{significant} but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp; \\cref{sec:32b-analysis}), and \\emph{exploratory} at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, pattern consistent with position mediation, below MDE; \\cref{sec:experiments}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Metric-induced layer selection bias.} We identify a metric-induced systematic bias: crossing-rate composite metrics systematically select shallow layers, reversing HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\\cref{sec:layer-results}).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Encoder-quality boundary with evidence gradient.} Against 384d MiniLM, the HS advantage is cross-validated at 7--8B (+20.1 to +21.9pp, HB corrected, nested CV), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, pattern consistent with position mediation). Against 1024d BGE-large, only OT-7B survives correction (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Negative results define method boundaries.} No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing at any evidence tier (\\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD/CUSUM fail on gradual transitions; Mahalanobis distance finds near-zero separation; RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (competitive on QwQ-32B, poor elsewhere); cross-model transfer fails under PCA alignment. Control task selectivity (11.1--16.8pp above chance; \\cref{app:control}) confirms genuine safety structure.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\noindent These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark for hidden-state safety monitoring and identify layer selection as a critical confound that future work must address.\n",
        "numLines": 21,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 21
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex"
  },
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "38f514e3-7909-4a95-8288-8ba43c67f678",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex",
        "content": "Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against MiniLM-384d (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; advantage is encoder-conditional and represents an upper bound); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are cross-validated (nested CV): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\\dagger$ ($^\\dagger$\\,sample-limited, $n \\leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \\leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.\n",
        "numLines": 2,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 2
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XFDTrEMK8kZCNWMT9hJ1U2`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{cross-validated}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers across all four models: L0 for R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep; \cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}) and OT-7B, L2 (3\%) for QwQ-32B, and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer---L63 (98\%) for both 32B models---consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The total exploration space spans 5 metrics $\times$ 7 window sizes $\times$ 28--64 layers (model-dependent); HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests, not this full search (\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness}). The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).\looseness=-1
14	
15	\begin{table}[t]
```

</details>

```text
95	
96	\subsection{Temporal Context}\label{sec:temporal}
97	
98	Temporal windowing ($W{>}1$) improves both modalities (\cref{fig:window_ablation}): at 7--8B, the modality advantage (+20.1 to +21.9pp) exceeds temporal improvement (+13pp). GRU models achieve comparable accuracy but higher FPR (\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:temporal_details}).\looseness=-1
99	
100	\begin{figure}[t]
101	\centering
102	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_forest_precision.pdf}
103	\caption{Forest plot of HS precision advantage over text probes. Blue: vs.\ MiniLM-384d; orange: vs.\ BGE-1024d. Solid markers: survives Holm-Bonferroni; hollow: does not. All 384d comparisons are significant (strong to qualified evidence); against BGE-1024d, only OT-7B (strong) survives correction.}
104	\label{fig:forest_precision}
105	\end{figure}
106	
107	CP-aligned probe score trajectories (\cref{fig:trajectory} in Appendix) confirm sharper HS transitions at the commitment point, with OT-7B showing the clearest separation and R1-32B the weakest.\looseness=-1
108	
109	\subsection{Negative Results}\label{sec:negative}
110	
111	No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing (\cref{tab:negative}). BOCPD~\citep{adams2007bayesian}/CUSUM fail (0\% detection on 3/4 models; \cref{app:bocpd_config}); Mahalanobis distance yields near-zero separation; RepE~\citep{zou2023representation} is model-dependent (QwQ-32B BA 0.756 vs.\ R1-32B 0.559); MLP probes overfit; CCS misaligns with temporal structure; cross-model transfer fails. These failures define four requirements: the signal is directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable. Supervised linear probes with principled layer selection form the minimal effective methodology (\cref{app:negative_details}).\looseness=-1
112	
```

BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian}/CUSUM fail (0\\% detection on 3/4 models; \\cref{app:bocpd_config}); Mahalanobis distance yields near-zero separation; RepE~\\citep{zou2023representation} is model-dependent (QwQ-32B BA 0.756 vs.\\ R1-32B 0.559); MLP probes overfit; CCS misaligns with temporal structure; cross-model transfer fails. These failures define four requirements: the signal is directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable. Supervised linear probes with principled layer selection form the minimal effective methodology (\\cref{app:negative_details}).\\looseness=-1\n",

```json
      "startLine": 95,
      "totalLines": 112
    }
```
